      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-ba-plate-routing",
        "exact_quote": "Let $P_i,Q_i\\subset\\mathbb R^2$ be finite families of closed rectangles, separately pairwise disjoint, and let assigned positive diagonal affine maps $F_i$ satisfy $F_i(P_i)\\subseteq Q_i$. Write their diagonal factors as $\\lambda_{i1}^*,\\lambda_{i2}^*>0$ with product one. There is a smooth compactly supported solenoidal unit-time field, zero on time collars, whose endpoint map is $(y,z)\\mapsto(F_i(y),z)$ on a neighborhood of each entire plate $P_i\\times\\{0\\}$. During the horizontal phase both planar factors interpolate linearly, while the normal factor compensates their product. Each planar coordinate of a point on the plate is its convex endpoint interpolation.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-ba-realization",
        "exact_quote": "Fix computable $\\nu>0$. Let $W$ be smooth, solenoidal, initially zero, with support in one compact subset of $\\mathbb R^3$ and every mixed derivative bounded. Then $$\\label{ba:residual}\n F_W=W_t+(W\\cdot\\nabla)W-\\nu\\Delta W$$ has the same support and derivative properties. The zero-data solution $(W,0)$ is unique among smooth solutions with $u\\in C_tH^2\\cap C_t^1L^2$, bounded $u$ on finite intervals, and $p\\in C_tH^1$. The corresponding torus assertion holds in the classical comparison class of Section <a href=\"#sec:model\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sec:model\">2</a>. A sum of curls in a torus chart gives both $W$ and $F_W$ mean zero. Finite formulas from the effective-data instances of the preceding constructions, finite loading, and repetition of one cycle give effective evaluation of every force derivative.",
        "source_id": "PM-376.9"

        "anchor": "stmt-thm-bb-direction39",
        "exact_quote": "<span id=\"thm:bb-aligned38\" label=\"thm:bb-aligned38\"></span>The direction recorder gives both of the following realizations.*\n\n1.  *On $\\mathbb R^3$, with its passive origin bit, there is a force periodic from time zero and a fixed-origin event $X_1(t;0)<-5$. On every implemented full box the first coordinate is a convex combination of its endpoint first coordinates.*\n\n2.  *On the unit torus, without the passive track, a force periodic after $t=1$ has fixed label $(1/8,1/8,1/4)$ and event $x_1\\in(1/2,7/8)\\pmod1$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-ba-loaded-torus",
        "exact_quote": "For every machine and finite input and every fixed computable $\\nu>0$, there is an effective smooth mean-zero force, one-periodic for $t\\ge1$ with bounded mixed derivatives, whose unique smooth zero-data solution with zero normalized pressure satisfies $$\\exists t\\ge0:\\ \\Phi_t(1/2,1/2,1/4)\n       \\in(1/16,1/4)\\times\\mathbb T^2\n \\quad\\Longleftrightarrow\\quad\\text{halting}.$$ Uniqueness holds in the classical class of Section <a href=\"#sec:model\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sec:model\">2</a>.",
        "source_id": "PM-376.9"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-ba-loaded-torus",
          "exact_quote": "For every machine and finite input and every fixed computable $\\nu>0$, there is an effective smooth mean-zero force, one-periodic for $t\\ge1$ with bounded mixed derivatives, whose unique smooth zero-data solution with zero normalized pressure satisfies $$\\exists t\\ge0:\\ \\Phi_t(1/2,1/2,1/4)\n       \\in(1/16,1/4)\\times\\mathbb T^2\n \\quad\\Longleftrightarrow\\quad\\text{halting}.$$ Uniqueness holds in the classical class of Section <a href=\"#sec:model\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sec:model\">2</a>.",
          "source_id": "PM-376.9"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "For every machine and finite input and every fixed computable $\\nu>0$, there is an effective smooth mean-zero force, one-periodic for $t\\ge1$ with bounded mixed derivatives, whose unique smooth zero-data solution with zero normalized pressure satisfies $$\\exists t\\ge0:\\ \\Phi_t(1/2,1/2,1/4)\n       \\in(1/16,1/4)\\times\\mathbb T^2\n \\quad\\Longleftrightarrow\\quad\\text{halting}.$$ Uniqueness holds in the classical class of Section <a href=\"#sec:model\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sec:model\">2</a>.",
      "explanation": "A one-interval loader followed by a repeated cycle gives a torus force whose particle enters a slab exactly upon halting.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-ba-periodic-torus",
        "exact_quote": "For every finite machine and input, and every fixed computable $\\nu>0$, there is an effective smooth mean-zero force on the unit flat torus, one-periodic from time zero, with all mixed derivatives bounded. Its zero-data solution has pressure zero, is unique in the classical class of Section <a href=\"#sec:model\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sec:model\">2</a>, and satisfies $$\\exists t\\ge0:\\ \\Phi_t(1/8,1/8,1/4)\n             \\in\\{5/8<X_1<7/8\\}\n \\quad\\Longleftrightarrow\\quad\\text{the machine halts}.$$ The one repeated cycle implements all instruction branches and its loading branch.",
        "source_id": "PM-376.9"
      },
      "evidence": [

        "anchor": "stmt-thm-bb-prefix29",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-ba-log-clock",
        "exact_quote": "Let $W(s,x)$ be a smooth solenoidal field for $s\\ge0$, initially zero, with every mixed derivative bounded and support in one compact set $K$. For fixed computable $\\nu>0$, set $$s(t)=\\log(1+t),\\quad \\rho(t)=(1+t)^{-1},\\quad\n U(t,x)=\\rho(t)W(s(t),x),\\quad\n F=U_t+(U\\cdot\\nabla)U-\\nu\\Delta U.$$ Every derivative $\\partial_t^k\\partial_x^\\alpha U$ and $\\partial_t^k\\partial_x^\\alpha F$ is uniformly $O((1+t)^{-1-k})$ and belongs to space-time $L^2$. Both supports lie in $K$. If $\\Xi$ is the material flow of $W$, that of $U$ is $\\Xi(s(t),a)$, so every fixed spatial reachability event is unchanged. Effective finite formulas for $W$ give effective formulas for $U,F$ and all derivatives.",
        "source_id": "PM-376.9"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-ba-log-clock",
          "exact_quote": "Let $W(s,x)$ be a smooth solenoidal field for $s\\ge0$, initially zero, with every mixed derivative bounded and support in one compact set $K$. Both supports lie in $K$. If $\\Xi$ is the material flow of $W$, that of $U$ is $\\Xi(s(t),a)$, so every fixed spatial reachability event is unchanged. Effective finite formulas for $W$ give effective formulas for $U,F$ and all derivatives.",
          "source_id": "PM-376.9"
        }
      ],

        "exact_quote": "For every finite machine and input, and every fixed computable $\\nu>0$, either the move-first recorder of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:bb-movefirst\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:bb-movefirst\">[lem:bb-movefirst]</a> or the six-rule recorder of Lemma <a href=\"#ba:six-compiler\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"ba:six-compiler\">16</a> yields effective smooth forcing on $\\mathbb R^3$ with one compact spatial support, all mixed derivatives bounded, and period one for $t\\ge1$, such that $$\\exists t\\ge0:\\ (\\Phi_t(0))_1<-1\n \\quad\\Longleftrightarrow\\quad\\text{halting}.$$ The zero-data solution is unique in Lemma <a href=\"#ba:realization\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"ba:realization\">21</a>’s class, has pressure zero and uniformly bounded energy.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-bb-mid-parking",
        "exact_quote": "<span id=\"prop:bb-mid-stream\" label=\"prop:bb-mid-stream\"></span>Both left-frontier tables have compact whole-space realizations, periodic after $t=1$, with fixed label zero and event $X_1(t;0)<-1/2$. The stopping version can use distinct-height stream functions and has uniqueness with pointwise-in-time $H^1$ comparison pressure. The continuing version transports full solid boxes by evacuation, reshaping and delivery, and represents every local step, including post-halt idle computation.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-ba-persistent-compiler",
        "exact_quote": "Let a machine have work alphabet $\\Gamma$, halt state $h$, and instructions $\\gamma=(q,a)\\mapsto(q^+_\\gamma,a^+_\\gamma,d_\\gamma)$, where $q\\ne h$ and $d_\\gamma\\in\\{-1,0,1\\}$. There is an effective finite partial one-head table with these properties. At the checkpoint after $n$ work steps, it has the correct work tape, state and head, a unique mark at that head, and a history pointer at $p_n=n+2$. Each target control determines the incoming displacement; the target control and written full cell symbol determine the source control and read full symbol on the entire partial domain. If the next work-head position is $w'=w_n+d_\\gamma$, the next checkpoint is reached after exactly $2(p_n-w')+4$ local steps.",
        "source_id": "PM-376.9"
      },

        "exact_quote": "The fresh-tail recorder has an effective compactly supported smooth realization on $\\mathbb R^3$, with zero initial velocity and force periodic after one unit of loading, whose event $\\exists t\\ge0:(\\Phi_t(0))_1<-1$ is equivalent to halting. A separate choice, with the same event and one compact support, satisfies for every $k\\ge0$ and spatial multi-index $\\alpha$ $$\\label{ba:fresh-decay}\n \\sup_x\\bigl(|\\partial_t^k\\partial_x^\\alpha u|+\n             |\\partial_t^k\\partial_x^\\alpha F|\\bigr)\n \\le C_{k,\\alpha}(1+t)^{-1-k}.$$ Every displayed derivative is space-time square-integrable. Both forces have the unique zero-pressure solution of Lemma <a href=\"#ba:realization\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"ba:realization\">21</a> at the fixed computable viscosity.",
        "source_id": "PM-376.9"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-ba-occupied-compiler",
        "exact_quote": "Let a machine have state set $Q$, halting set $H$, alphabet $\\Gamma$, and instructions $\\tau=(q,a)\\mapsto(q^+_\\tau,w_\\tau,d_\\tau)$. There is an effective partial injective map on head-relative tapes whose domain depends only on the control and full cells at offsets $-1,0,1$. With the initialization below, occupied history at checkpoint $n$ consists exactly of the absolute indices less than $F_n=n+2$. A work step ending at head position $j$ takes exactly $2(F_n-j)+2$ local steps. Halting at a checkpoint, including the initial one, is equivalent to halting of the original machine.",

        "anchor": "stmt-ba-occupied-torus",
        "exact_quote": "For each machine and finite input, at any fixed positive computable viscosity there is an effective smooth mean-zero force on the unit flat three-torus, with bounded mixed derivatives and period one for $t\\ge1$, whose smooth zero-data solution has pressure zero and satisfies $$\\exists t\\ge0:\\ X(t;[(-1/16,0,0)])\\in\n \\{[x]:x_1\\bmod1\\in(0,1/2)\\}\n \\quad\\Longleftrightarrow\\quad\\text{the machine halts}.$$ The pressure is normalized to mean zero, and the solution is unique in the classical torus comparison class of Section <a href=\"#sec:model\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sec:model\">2</a>.",

        "exact_quote": "An effective smooth force on $\\mathbb R^3$, periodic from time zero, with one compact spatial support and bounded mixed derivatives, realizes the fixed event $$\\exists t\\ge0:\\quad X(t;(2,0,0))\\in(-2,-1)\\times(0,1)\\times(-1,1)\n \\quad\\Longleftrightarrow\\quad\\text{halting}.$$ Velocity is unique in the class $u\\in C([0,T];H^4)\\cap C^1([0,T];H^2)$, $\\nabla p\\in C([0,T];L^2)$ for every finite $T$, allowing a distributional pressure gradient.         "anchor": "stmt-bc-halfline-event",
        "exact_quote": "On $\\mathbb T^3$, the fixed label $(1/8,1/4,1/4)$ and strip $2/3<x_1<15/16$ realize halting by a general mean-zero force, with zero initial velocity and pressure, bounded mixed derivatives and period one after time one.         "anchor": "stmt-ba-bounded-box-tests",
        "exact_quote": "Fix a positive computable viscosity $\\nu$. A deterministic one-tape machine and finite input effectively determine each of the following general body forces on $[0,\\infty)\\times\\mathbb R^3$. Both have zero initial velocity, a unique global smooth velocity in <a href=\"#ba:euclidean-comparison-class\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ba:euclidean-comparison-class\">[ba:euclidean-comparison-class]</a>, and constructed pressure zero.*\n\n1.  *The force and velocity have one fixed compact spatial support, all mixed derivatives bounded, and period one for $t\\ge1$. The particle from $0$ enters $(-5,-2)\\times(-1,2)\\times(-1,1)$ exactly when the machine halts.*\n\n\n2.  *The force and velocity have one fixed compact spatial support and, for all $k\\ge0$ and spatial multi-indices $\\alpha$, satisfy $$\\label{ba:eighteen-decay}\n     \\|\\partial_t^k\\partial_x^\\alpha u(t)\\|_\\infty+\n     \\|\\partial_t^k\\partial_x^\\alpha f(t)\\|_\\infty\n     \\le C_{k,\\alpha}(1+t)^{-1-k}.$$ The particle from $(2,0,0)$ enters $(-1/2,3/2)\\times(-1/2,3/2)\\times(-1/2,1/2)$ exactly when the machine halts. In particular $f\\in L^2([0,\\infty);H^m(\\mathbb R^3))$ for every integer $m\\ge0$.*\n\n*The supports and constants may depend on the machine and input. The labels and observers do not. All force derivatives admit evaluation to any prescribed rational error by a finite program.",
        "source_id": "PM-376.9"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-ba-torus-plates",
        "exact_quote": "Fix a positive computable $\\nu$. Every deterministic one-tape machine with finitely specified tape, blank outside a finite set, effectively determines a smooth mean-zero general force on $\\mathbb T^3=\\mathbb R^3/\\mathbb Z^3$, with bounded mixed derivatives and period one for $t\\ge1$. Its zero-data Navier–Stokes solution is globally smooth and unique among smooth solutions, with pressure normalized to mean zero and constructed pressure zero. For $P_*=(1/4,1/2,1/4)$ and $X(t)=\\Phi_t(P_*)$, the event $$\\label{ba:torus-plate-event}\n \\exists t\\ge0:\\quad X_1(t)\\pmod1\\in(1/2,7/8)$$ is equivalent to halting.*\n\n\n*A separate effective force has the same zero data, event, uniqueness and mean-zero property, is supported in one compact sub-box of $(0,1)^3$, has all mixed derivatives bounded, and satisfies $$\\label{ba:cube-rate}\n \\|\\widehat f(t,\\cdot)\\|_\\infty=O((1+t)^{-2/3}).$$ In particular $\\widehat f\\in L^2([0,\\infty)\\times\\mathbb T^3)$.",
        "source_id": "PM-376.9"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-ba-torus-plates",
          "exact_quote": "Fix a positive computable $\\nu$. Its zero-data Navier–Stokes solution is globally smooth and unique among smooth solutions, with pressure normalized to mean zero and constructed pressure zero. For $P_*=(1/4,1/2,1/4)$ and $X(t)=\\Phi_t(P_*)$, the event $$\\label{ba:torus-plate-event}\n \\exists t\\ge0:\\quad X_1(t)\\pmod1\\in(1/2,7/8)$$ is equivalent to halting.*\n\n\n*A separate effective force has the same zero data, event, uniqueness and mean-zero property, is supported in one compact sub-box of $(0,1)^3$, has all mixed derivatives bounded, and satisfies $$\\label{ba:cube-rate}\n \\|\\widehat f(t,\\cdot)\\|_\\infty=O((1+t)^{-2/3}).$$ In particular $\\widehat f\\in L^2([0,\\infty)\\times\\mathbb T^3)$.",
          "source_id": "PM-376.9"
        }

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-ba-zero-blank-code",
        "exact_quote": "Let an alphabet of size $m>1$ have digits $0,\\ldots,m-1$, with full blank digit zero. Set $B=m+1$, $\\kappa=(m-1)/m$, and $$\\operatorname{val}(a_0a_1\\cdots)=\\sum_{r\\ge0}a_rB^{-r-1},\\qquad\n I_a=\\frac{a+[0,\\kappa]}B,\\qquad I_{a,b}=\\frac{a+I_b}B.$$ The code is injective, takes values in $[0,\\kappa]$, and its distinct one-letter and two-letter cylinders have positive rational gaps. For a deterministic partial table with target-state-determined incoming direction and incoming rule determined by target state and written symbol, separated state translates of $[0,\\kappa]^2$ yield separately disjoint full source and target rectangles by the following maps in state-relative coordinates: $$\\label{ba:zero-digit-maps}\n \\begin{array}{c|c|c|c}\n d&\\text{source}&(\\xi',\\eta')&\\text{target}\\\\\\hline\n 1&[0,\\kappa]\\times I_a&((b+\\xi)/B,B\\eta-a)&I_b\\times[0,\\kappa]\\\\\n 0&[0,\\kappa]\\times I_a&(\\xi,\\eta+(b-a)/B)&[0,\\kappa]\\times I_b\\\\\n -1&I_c\\times I_a&(B\\xi-c,c/B+(b+B\\eta-a)/B^2)&\n                                      [0,\\kappa]\\times I_{c,b}.\n \\end{array}$$ The last row is split over all $c$. Each map has positive diagonal linear part of determinant one and acts exactly on the full rectangle.",
        "source_id": "PM-376.9"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-ba-zero-blank-code",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-ba-periodic-parking",
        "exact_quote": "Fix a positive computable $\\nu$. A deterministic one-tape machine and finite input word in cells $0,\\ldots,L-1$, with other cells blank and initial head zero, effectively determine a general body force on $\\mathbb R^3$ extending smoothly and one-periodically to all real times. It has uniformly compact spatial support and bounded mixed derivatives. Its zero-data solution has a unique global smooth velocity in <a href=\"#ba:euclidean-comparison-class\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ba:euclidean-comparison-class\">[ba:euclidean-comparison-class]</a>, with constructed pressure zero, and $$\\exists t\\ge0:\\quad (\\Phi_t((-2,0,0)))_1>0\n \\quad\\Longleftrightarrow\\quad\\text{the machine halts}.$$ The fixed label is already the initial code; periodicity begins at zero.",
        "source_id": "PM-376.9"
      },
      "evidence": [

        "anchor": "stmt-ba-bounded-box-euclidean",
        "exact_quote": "For every machine and finite input, and fixed positive computable viscosity, there is an effective smooth force on $\\mathbb R^3$ with uniformly compact spatial support, bounded mixed derivatives and period one for $t\\ge1$, whose zero-data solution satisfies $$\\exists t\\ge0:\\ X(t;(2,0,0))\\in(0,1)\\times(0,1)\\times(-1,1)\n \\quad\\Longleftrightarrow\\quad\\text{the machine halts}.$$ The constructed solution is smooth with pressure zero.         "exact_quote": "Let $D_i,S_i$ be finite families of closed rational axis-aligned boxes in $\\mathbb R^3$ with positive side lengths, pairwise disjoint within each family.         "exact_quote": "Let the source and target families be finite collections of full rational axis-aligned solid boxes, each family pairwise disjoint. <a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **effective field** ([source](#pm-h-4)): An effective field is a finite program that evaluates every requested mixed derivative at computably supplied arguments to any prescribed rational error and computes the bounds used in the proof.
- **material label and path** ([source](#pm-h-4)): A material label $a$ has path $\dot\Phi_t(a)=u(t,\Phi_t(a))$, $\Phi_0(a)=a$.
- **residual force operator** ([source](#sec:model)): Write $\mathcal F_\nu[U]=U_t+(U\cdot\nabla)U-\nu\Delta U$.
- **one-cell table** ([source](#pm-h-13)): A partial one-cell table sends a control and a scanned letter to a new control, a written letter, and a displacement.
- **incoming conditions** ([source](#pm-h-13)): Its two incoming conditions are: the new control determines the displacement, and the new control together with the written letter determines the entire old control and read letter.

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-pressure-orthogonality** [claim](#stmt-lem-b-gradient) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-b-gradient): Fourier transform and Plancherel -> zero pairing
- **step-energy-comparison** [claim](#stmt-lem-p2-realization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-p2-realization): Energy inequality for the difference with cutoffs and integrating factor -> uniqueness in each comparison class
- **step-curl-localization** [claim](#stmt-lem-bb-curl) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-bb-curl): Curl of a cutoff vector potential and ODE uniqueness on the plateau -> solenoidal field realizing the motions
- **step-pulse-residual** [claim](#stmt-prop-bb-realization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-bb-realization): Residual force of the concatenated pulses -> force and unique solution
- **step-lift-parking** [claim](#stmt-lem-bb-lift-parking) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-bb-lift-parking): Private heights or evacuation then delivery, applying the curl lemma phase by phase -> unit-time box maps
- **step-box-routing** [claim](#stmt-lem-bb-balanced-box-routing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-bb-balanced-box-routing): Expansion, parking, obstacle-cube polygonal detours and contraction -> time-one box maps
- **step-one-cell-rectangles** [claim](#stmt-lem-bb-mid-prefix) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-bb-mid-prefix): Gapped radix coding of tape streams and incoming-condition separation -> separated reciprocal rectangle maps
- **step-move-first-recorder** [claim](#stmt-lem-bb-mid-right-compiler) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-bb-mid-right-compiler): Mark, forward scan, record, backward scan with inductive invariant -> history recorder table
- **step-three-stack** [claim](#stmt-thm-bb-balanced-three-stack) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-bb-balanced-three-stack): Balanced prefix rules, odd-digit box coding, routing and intermediate-time exclusion -> force with halting event
- **step-torus-move-first** [claim](#stmt-thm-bb-torus33) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-bb-torus33): Chart placement, three-phase lift with exponential scale, loader -> torus realization
- **step-log-clock** [claim](#stmt-ba-log-clock) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ba-log-clock): Time change with derivative identity by induction -> decaying force
- **step-ordered-extraction** [claim](#stmt-bc-ordered) / [proof](#proof-stmt-bc-ordered): Extraction order by right endpoint into deep storage columns -> full box transport with height guard

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-prefix-slope**: Replacing a prefix of length r by a prefix of length s in a positional code with base B acts on the coded coordinate as an affine map with slope B^{r-s}.
- **inv-code-identity**: The code of a prefix followed by a tail equals the code of the prefix plus a shifted code of the tail, so the affine rule sends the code of a source word with tail to the code of the target word with the same tail.
- **inv-det-one**: A rule that preserves the sum of the three prefix lengths has a diagonal affine map of determinant one; otherwise the determinant is a power of B given by the length difference.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-unbalanced-rule**: Feeding a rule whose output prefixes are shorter or longer in total than its input prefixes and expecting a volume-preserving box map. -> The product of the three slopes is a nonzero power of B different from one, so the map is not incompressible and cannot be realized by the divergence-free transport.
- **anti-harness-as-run**: Reading the arithmetic harness as a simulation of the machine run or of the fluid flow. -> The harness checks only the exact code identity and determinant of one rule; it says nothing about halting, intermediate times, or the force.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. The prefix-replacement rule of the balanced three-stack construction is a finite explicit procedure: its affine map and determinant are computed exactly from finite words, so a runnable reference and an independent oracle exist. Only this rule bookkeeping is executed, not the fluid transport.

```python
# INPUTS: stdin; line 1 is the base B (integer >= 3); then three lines, one per stack, each with three comma-separated digit lists (a dash means empty): input prefix, output prefix, finite tail.
# PRECONDITIONS: digits are integers in [0, B-2]; exactly three stack lines.
# OUTPUTS: line det=<exact fraction>, then three lines with the image code of each stack under the affine rule.
# TIME: linear in the total length of the words.
# SPACE: linear in the total length of the words.
# ARITHMETIC: exact rationals (fractions.Fraction), no floating point.
# FAILURE: malformed input raises an exception and gives a nonzero exit status.
import sys
from fractions import Fraction


def e(w, B):
    return sum(Fraction(d, B**(j+1)) for j, d in enumerate(w))


def parse(tok):
    return [] if tok == '-' else [int(x) for x in tok.split(',')]


def main():
    lines = sys.stdin.read().split('\n')
    B = int(lines[0].strip())
    det = Fraction(1)
    ys = []
    for j in range(3):
        a, b, t = [parse(x) for x in lines[1+j].split()]
        x = e(a+t, B)
        scale = Fraction(B)**(len(a)-len(b))
        y = e(b, B)+scale*(x-e(a, B))
        det *= scale
        ys.append(y)
    print('det='+str(det))
    for y in ys:
        print(y)


main()
```

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | The paper defines an effective field as a finite program that evaluates every mixed derivative at approximate arguments and computes its own bounds. That is a concrete library contract: a field evaluator whose coefficients come only from the finite machine table and input, not from running the machine. |
| games_simulation | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | Because the paper prescribes a smooth solenoidal velocity and reads off the body force by the residual identity, the pair (velocity, zero pressure) is an exact manufactured solution of the forced Navier-Stokes system with zero initial data. A flow solver can be checked against it, with uniqueness only in the stated comparison classes. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No stand-alone tool, command or interface is specified for the constructions; the paper gives mathematical procedures and proofs only. |
| algorithms | True | strong | adaptable | inferred | The box-transport theorem is an explicit finite procedure: scale centers apart, change shapes, park every box, then deliver along rational polygonal paths that keep a fixed clearance from stationary boxes, and finally contract. Each step uses rational inequalities and positive margins, so the schedule is computable from the box data. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The three stacks are mathematical infinite words encoded as real numbers in gapped radix expansions, not a bounded in-memory container with operations and costs. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The encoded trajectories are exact real trajectories, so there is no finite-precision datapath or fixed-point format that the results address. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Nothing in the paper concerns secrecy, adversaries or hardness assumptions; the machine is public and the observation event is deterministic. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | There is no learning problem, dataset, loss or trained model; the force is built directly from a finite instruction table. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No objective, constraint set or optimality claim appears; routing only needs a feasible collision-free schedule, not a minimal one. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | A single global force drives one flow with no message passing, replicas or fault model; periodicity is a time property of one field. |
| other (theory of computation and dynamical systems) | True | moderate | inspirational | inferred | The central statement is a theoretical embedding of machine halting into a fluid-particle event: from rest, a fixed particle enters a fixed open set exactly when a machine halts. It serves as a source of undecidability-type conclusions about observation of forced flows. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-no-regularity-theorem** (formal_scope): The paper builds explicit smooth velocities and forces. It makes no regularity or well-posedness claim for arbitrary fluid data, so the constructions say nothing about general initial data or forcing. Blocked use: ['Citing the results as a regularity or blow-up theorem for generic data', 'Using the explicit solutions to bound the behaviour of unrelated forced flows']
- **limit-box-families-disjoint** (hypothesis): The box-routing theorem needs rational solid boxes with positive half-widths, with source family and target family each pairwise disjoint. Source and target families may overlap each other, but boxes inside one family may not. Blocked use: ['Routing boxes that overlap within the same family', 'Routing degenerate boxes with zero half-width under this theorem', 'Using non-rational endpoint data and still claiming the effective procedure']
- **limit-balanced-determinant-one** (hypothesis): Each assigned diagonal map must have product of its scale factors equal to one. A rule whose prefix lengths are not balanced has a different determinant and is not incompressible. Blocked use: ['Realizing a volume-changing prefix rule by the divergence-free routing', 'Dropping the history or blank-supply step that restores balance']
- **limit-intermediate-time-observer-guard** (edge_case): Matching the endpoint codes at integer times does not by itself give a material event. A separate geometric bound is required to exclude visits to the observer between rule times, and each construction supplies its own guard. Blocked use: ['Deducing the halting equivalence from integer-time codes alone', 'Transferring an observer guard from one routing schedule to another without re-checking the intermediate paths']
- **limit-full-domain-inverse** (hypothesis): Sharing a recorder across constructions requires an inverse on the whole allowed domain of tapes, not only agreement along the initialized run. Tables that differ on unvisited tapes give different source and image cylinders. Blocked use: ['Substituting a recorder that is injective only along the executed computation', 'Reusing a rectangle-separation argument for a table whose guards differ on arbitrary allowed tapes']
- **limit-declared-comparison-class** (formal_scope): Uniqueness of the constructed solution holds only in the comparison class named by each theorem. The three-stack theorem fixes a Sobolev class with bounded velocity and gradient and pressure modulo constants in a first-order Sobolev space. Blocked use: ['Claiming uniqueness among all weak or Leray-Hopf solutions', 'Using a different pressure class than the one declared for the construction']
- **limit-no-simultaneous-guarantees** (formal_scope): The alternatives fix their own label, observer, support, pressure class and time dependence. They are separate constructions, not properties of one force. Blocked use: ['Combining periodicity from time zero, decay and a pressure class from different theorems for a single force', 'Assuming one force realizes several observers at once']
- **limit-gradient-only-pressure** (formal_scope): Pressure norms are not silently imposed. Applications that only control a pressure gradient keep that weaker condition, and the pressure is then determined only up to a function of time. Blocked use: ['Assuming an integrable pressure representative in a gradient-only application', 'Reading a pressure normalization into a result stated without one']
- **limit-evaluator-never-runs-machine** (nonconstructive): The effective field is a finite program built from the finite table and input. Evaluating it does not first compute the selected machine run, so it cannot be used to decide halting by evaluation. Blocked use: ['Using field evaluation as a shortcut to simulate or decide the machine', 'Expecting the program size to track the length of the computation']
- **limit-exact-real-trajectories** (approximation): The encoded trajectories are exact real trajectories. No uniform precision tolerance for an unbounded computation is asserted, so rounding-based implementations carry no guarantee. Blocked use: ['Running the construction in fixed-precision arithmetic and expecting the halting equivalence', 'Quoting a robustness or noise margin for the encoded tape']
- **limit-viscosity-parameter** (hypothesis): Algorithmic statements concern computable viscosity. The same formulas hold for any positive viscosity but evaluate only relative to that parameter. Blocked use: ['Claiming effectivity for a viscosity given by a non-computable real', 'Treating the evaluation as independent of the viscosity parameter']
- **limit-chart-rescaling-viscosity** (hypothesis): When a chart is rescaled the residual force must be recomputed at the fixed physical viscosity. The rescaled equation is not identified with one of different viscosity. Blocked use: ['Reusing the unscaled force after a change of spatial chart', 'Reading a rescaled construction as a solution at another viscosity']
- **limit-projection-changes-pressure** (formal_scope): Projection of the torus force changes the pressure and supplies no compact support on Euclidean space. It is a different choice from the direct zero-pressure construction. Blocked use: ['Assuming the projected force keeps pressure zero', 'Using projection to obtain compact support on whole space']
- **limit-clock-must-be-onto** (hypothesis): A time change used to produce decaying forces must tend to infinity. A clock that covers only a finite amount of machine time would not preserve the halting event. Blocked use: ['Slowing the clock by a bounded reparametrization and keeping the event equivalence', 'Applying the decay lemma to a template with a clock that stalls']
- **limit-slowed-force-not-periodic** (edge_case): Slowed forces obtained by the logarithmic clock have the stated integrability and decay, but no periodicity in physical time is claimed for them. Blocked use: ['Expecting a decaying force to be periodic after loading', 'Using the slowed template where a periodic drive is required']
- **limit-eulerian-detector-separate** (counterexample_scope): The velocity-integral detector of the companion work is a separate force and observation regime. Its diffusion tails give no uniform spatial support, so it does not inherit the material-test conclusions proved here. Blocked use: ['Transferring compact-support or exact-trajectory conclusions to the Eulerian detector', 'Treating the two observation regimes as one theorem']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We realize finite positive diagonal affine maps of determinant one by effective smooth incompressible flows on neighborhoods of entire closed rational solid boxes. The source and target families are each disjoint, but may overlap each other. The construction uses localized curls, evacuation to storage and obstacle detours. A balanced three-stack recorder then assigns every machine and finite input a smooth Navier–Stokes force with one compact spatial support, periodic after a loading interval, at any fixed positive computable viscosity. The fluid starts at rest, and one fixed particle enters one fixed open cube exactly when the machine halts. Further constructions give fixed torus charts, periodicity from time zero, alternative history guards and bounded or slab observers. Onto slow clocks yield separate decaying forces. Each construction includes an all-time observation proof, effective derivative bounds and a stated pressure comparison class.

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

A finite instruction can prescribe a volume-preserving affine map on a box. A fluid realization must do more: it must move every point of that box, preserve incompressibility outside it, and leave the other instructions available. Source boxes and destination boxes can overlap. Moreover, a particle used to report the answer must avoid the observation region throughout every nonhalting transition. We study these requirements together.

Our geometric result is Theorem <a href="#lem:bb-balanced-box-routing" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:bb-balanced-box-routing">8</a>. It extends any finite collection of positive diagonal determinant-one maps between separately disjoint rational solid boxes to the time-one map of an effective smooth compactly supported incompressible velocity. The maps hold on neighborhoods of the entire closed boxes. The proof separates centers, changes shapes, evacuates the sources to storage, and delivers boxes along paths avoiding everything still occupied. Positive margins make the construction effective and permit additional observation guards.

The first computational application, Theorem <a href="#thm:bb-balanced-three-stack" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:bb-balanced-three-stack">9</a>, uses all three coordinates for symbolic data. Three stacks retain a tape and its execution history. Every local instruction preserves the total length of the prefixes it replaces, so its affine map has determinant one. A fixed particle, initially in a fluid at rest, enters the fixed cube $(-1,2)^3$ exactly when the encoded machine halts. The force is smooth, compactly supported in space and periodic after a one-unit loading interval. Its prescription uses the finite rule table, not the executed computation. This application completes the main construction before we vary initialization, history guards and observers.

The passage from a symbolic instruction to a fluid observation begins with retained history, which makes the instruction injective on its entire domain of tapes, not just along the chosen computation. A rule specifies finite prefixes and leaves their infinite tails free; gapped radix expansions place the codes in separately disjoint closed source and target boxes. Prefix replacement extends to an affine map of each whole box, which smooth incompressible transport then realizes. An additional geometric bound must exclude unintended visits to the observer between successive rule times. Finally, the prescribed velocity determines a body force, and an energy comparison identifies the resulting solution in the declared class. Each later variant changes one or more of these steps while retaining this separation of tasks.

The remaining constructions answer more specific questions. Two tape coordinates can be thickened into solid boxes, or their planar scale changes can be compensated in the normal direction. A temporary mark, a persistent mark, an explicit frontier and a boundary between occupied and unused history give different partial instruction maps. We prove their inverses on their full domains because this is what separates the target boxes. A bounded observation box permits horizontal motion above it; a slab needs a coordinate bound throughout that motion. Storage and obstacle detours supply further alternatives. Loading once gives eventual periodicity, whereas placing the initial code at the fixed label or repeating a loading branch gives periodicity from time zero. Onto slow clocks give separate decaying forces with derivative norms square-integrable in time.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### Prior work and the role of forcing

The computational starting point is Turing’s finite machine model (Turing 1936--1937) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[Turing1936](#ref-Turing1936)]. Landauer’s discussion of retained intermediate information (Landauer 1961, sec. 3) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[Landauer1961](#ref-Landauer1961)] and Bennett’s explicit history recording (Bennett 1973, Eqs. (9)–(11)) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[Bennett1973](#ref-Bennett1973)] explain how an irreversible computation can be embedded in an invertible mechanism. We use that principle, but prove the finite guarded tables and their complete inverses here. No complexity bound or history-erasure procedure from those works is asserted for these tables.

Moore’s generalized shifts turn symbolic updates into affine operations on positional expansions (Moore 1990, 1991) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[Moore1990](#ref-Moore1990), [Moore1991](#ref-Moore1991)]. Our gapped expansions serve the same purpose. Moore also realizes invertible generalized shifts by smooth planar diffeomorphisms and three-dimensional flows (Moore 1991, sec. 6, Theorem 12 and its corollary) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[Moore1991](#ref-Moore1991)]. Cardona, Miranda, Peralta-Salas and Presas extend the affine maps on neighborhoods of separated planar blocks to an area-preserving disk diffeomorphism, using contraction, transport, expansion and Moser’s area correction (Cardona et al. 2021, Proposition 5.1) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[CardonaMirandaPeraltaSalasPresas2021](#ref-CardonaMirandaPeraltaSalasPresas2021)]. This is a geometric predecessor of the routing problem here. Our local curl formulas implement positive diagonal determinant-one motions in three dimensions directly; the proof supplies effective support and neighborhood bounds and controls observation at intermediate times.

Computational universality has been established for stationary Euler flows with adapted geometry (Cardona et al. 2021) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[CardonaMirandaPeraltaSalasPresas2021](#ref-CardonaMirandaPeraltaSalasPresas2021)]. Cardona, Miranda and Peralta-Salas also construct universal Euclidean Beltrami flows, with infinite energy, and robust simulations of tape-bounded machines by unforced Navier–Stokes flows on a flat torus (Cardona, Miranda, and Peralta-Salas 2023, arXiv version 3, Theorem 1 and Remark 29) <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[CardonaMirandaPeraltaSalas2023Beltrami](#ref-CardonaMirandaPeraltaSalas2023Beltrami)]. Dyhr, González-Prieto, Miranda and Peralta-Salas obtain stationary unforced Navier–Stokes universality using an adapted metric and the Hodge Laplacian (Dyhr et al. 2026, Theorem A) <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[DyhrGonzalezPrietoMirandaPeraltaSalas2026](#ref-DyhrGonzalezPrietoMirandaPeraltaSalas2026)].

Here the ambient metric is fixed and flat, the viscosity is prescribed, and the initial velocity is zero. The machine is placed in an external body force. Given an explicit solenoidal velocity $U$, the identity $f=U_t+(U\cdot\nabla)U-\nu\Delta U$ supplies that force. The mathematical work is the effective construction of $U$ and its all-time observation, followed by uniqueness in the stated comparison class. We do not assert a regularity theorem for arbitrary fluid data.

The companion (OpenAI 2026a) <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[OpenAI379Shear](#ref-OpenAI379Shear)] gives an entry construction using solenoidal shears, and (OpenAI 2026b) <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[OpenAI379Sheets](#ref-OpenAI379Sheets)] treats prefix instructions and other planar or normal-compensated realizations. They explain adjacent geometric choices; the proofs of the present full-box mechanisms are given here. The final input-offset application explicitly imports the planar processor and spatial clock of (OpenAI 2026c) <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[OpenAI379StationaryA2](#ref-OpenAI379StationaryA2)], with the exact parameters and conclusions stated in Section <a href="#bc:input-offset-section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="bc:input-offset-section">15.2</a>. The companion (OpenAI 2026d) <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[OpenAI379EulerianC](#ref-OpenAI379EulerianC)] detects computation from an integral of the velocity field on $\mathbb R^2\times\mathbb T$ via an advection–diffusion estimate. The end of Section <a href="#sec:bb-initialization" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:bb-initialization">8</a> compares that event with the path of one material particle studied here.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### Equation, effectiveness and reading path

Throughout $\nu>0$ is fixed, $\mathbb T=\mathbb R/\mathbb Z$, and the fluid equation on $\mathbb R^3$ or the flat unit torus is <a id="eq:box-NS"></a>$$\label{eq:box-NS}
 u_t+(u\cdot\nabla)u=-\nabla p+\nu\Delta u+f,
 \qquad \mathop{\mathrm{div}}u=0,\qquad u(0)=0.$$ A material label $a$ has path $\dot\Phi_t(a)=u(t,\Phi_t(a))$, $\Phi_0(a)=a$. We also write $X(t;a)=\Phi_t(a)$. The smooth constructed velocities have global material flows. On the torus pressure is normalized to have mean zero. Whole-space pressure assumptions are specified in Section <a href="#sec:model" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:model">2</a> and in the individual statements. Unless explicitly projected, the force is a general body force and the constructed pressure is zero.

An effective field is a finite program that evaluates every requested mixed derivative at computably supplied arguments to any prescribed rational error and computes the bounds used in the proof. The finite machine and its finite input determine the coefficients, cutoffs and routing schedule. Evaluating the field cannot first compute the selected machine run. All algorithmic statements concern computable $\nu$; the same formulas are valid for arbitrary positive $\nu$, with evaluation relative to that parameter. The encoded trajectories are exact real trajectories. No uniform precision tolerance for an unbounded computation is asserted.

Section <a href="#sec:model" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:model">2</a> states the analytic comparison tools. Section <a href="#sec:bb-boxes" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:bb-boxes">3</a> proves the box motions, and Section <a href="#sec:bb-balanced-three-stack" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:bb-balanced-three-stack">4</a> gives the balanced-stack application. Sections <a href="#sec:bb-frontiers" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:bb-frontiers">5</a> and <a href="#ba:codes" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="ba:codes">6</a> establish reusable recorders and full closed-rectangle coding; Section <a href="#ba:geometry" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="ba:geometry">7</a> supplies the common interpolation formulas. The subsequent constructions can be read according to the requirement being changed:

<div class="center">

| Choice                              | Where it is developed                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
|:------------------------------------|:----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| Initialization and time periodicity | Section <a href="#sec:bb-initialization" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:bb-initialization">8</a> compares a separate loader, direct placement of the initial code, and a loading branch repeated in every period. An untargeted start square permits the last choice.                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| History domains                     | Sections <a href="#ba:marked-routes" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="ba:marked-routes">10</a> and <a href="#sec:bb-guarded" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:bb-guarded">11</a> distinguish persistent marks, fresh history and neighboring-cell guards. Their inverses on arbitrary allowed tapes determine the separated image boxes.                                                                                                        |
| Observer geometry                   | Section <a href="#ba:ordered-routes" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="ba:ordered-routes">12</a> treats bounded boxes, slabs and half-spaces. Sections <a href="#sec:solid-routing" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:solid-routing">13</a> and <a href="#sec:torus-routing" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:torus-routing">14</a> develop storage, detours and fixed torus charts with the corresponding intermediate-time bounds. |
| Decay and stationarity              | The logarithmic clock in Section <a href="#ba:euclidean" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="ba:euclidean">9</a> produces separate decaying forces. Section <a href="#sec:clocks" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:clocks">15</a> treats further profile choices, including the imported input-offset processor.                                                                                                                                   |

</div>

The five whole-space comparison classes are stated in Lemma <a href="#lem:b-comparison" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:b-comparison">[lem:b-comparison]</a>. Each alternative fixes its own label, observer, support, pressure class and time dependence; these are separate constructions rather than simultaneous guarantees for one force.

<a id="sec:model"></a>
### Analytic classes and changes of physical coordinates

The geometric constructions prescribe a smooth velocity first. This section records exactly which other solutions are excluded by the energy argument. Pressure assumptions are kept separate: continuity in a pressure norm, a norm at each individual time, and an integrable pressure gradient are different conditions.

Write $\mathcal F_\nu[U]=U_t+(U\cdot\nabla)U-\nu\Delta U$. All noncompact results below use $\mathbb R^3$; their velocities and forces have one fixed compact spatial support unless the individual statement says otherwise. This property concerns the constructed solution, not a competing solution. On $[0,T]$, write $CH^k=C([0,T];H^k(\mathbb R^3))$ and $C^1L^2=C^1([0,T];L^2(\mathbb R^3))$. Spatial constants in pressure may depend on time.

<a id="stmt-lem-b-gradient"></a>
<div id="lem:b-gradient" class="lemma">

**Lemma 1** (The pressure term without a pressure norm). *If $w,g\in L^2(\mathbb R^3;\mathbb R^3)$ satisfy $\operatorname{div}w=0$ and $\operatorname{curl}g=0$ in distributions, then $\langle w,g\rangle_{L^2}=0$. In particular, any distributional gradient $g=\nabla p\in L^2$ has zero pairing with $w$, without an assumption that $p\in L^2$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-b-gradient"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-b-gradient</summary>

*Proof.* An $L^2$ field is a tempered distribution. The differential identities, initially tested on compactly supported smooth functions, extend to Schwartz tests by multiplying by cutoffs tending to one and using $L^2$ convergence of the tests and their first derivatives. Fourier transformation gives $\xi\cdot\widehat w=0$ and $\xi_j\widehat g_k-\xi_k\widehat g_j=0$. These are identities of locally integrable functions, so they hold almost everywhere. Away from $\xi=0$, $\widehat g$ is parallel to $\xi$ and $\widehat w$ is perpendicular to it. Their Hermitian product is zero. The exceptional point has measure zero; Plancherel proves the assertion. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-lem-p2-realization"></a>
<div id="lem:p2-realization" class="lemma">

**Lemma 2** (Residual realization and separate comparison classes). *<span id="lem:b-comparison" label="lem:b-comparison"></span>Let $\nu>0$ and let $U$ be a prescribed smooth divergence-free velocity on $[0,\infty)\times\mathbb R^3$, with $U(0)=0$. On each finite interval, assume $U\in CH^2\cap C^1L^2$ and $U,\nabla U$ are bounded. Put $f=\mathcal F_\nu[U]$. Then $(U,0)$ is a solution. Its velocity is unique in each of the following separately stated comparison classes, with the same force and initial data:*

1.  *Smooth classical $v\in CH^2\cap C^1L^2$, with bounded $v$, and a pressure representative $p\in CH^1$.*

2.  *Smooth classical $v\in CH^2\cap C^1L^2$, with bounded $v,\nabla v$, and an $H^1$ pressure representative at each time, with no required time continuity in that pressure norm.*

3.  *Strong solutions with $v\in CH^4\cap C^1H^2$ and distributional $\nabla p\in CL^2$.*

4.  *Smooth classical $v\in CH^2\cap C^1L^2$, with bounded $v,\nabla v$, and a pressure representative $p\in CL^2$.*

5.  *Smooth classical $v\in CH^2\cap C^1L^2$, with bounded $v,\nabla v$, without an additional integrability or normalization condition on pressure.*

*The pressure gradient is unique. An $L^2$ pressure representative, when specified, is zero; otherwise pressure is determined up to a function of time. The reference material flow is a smooth diffeomorphism at every finite time and exists for every finite forward time.*

*On a flat torus $\mathbb T_L^3$, the same conclusion holds in the classical class where $v,v_t$, spatial derivatives through order two, and $p,\nabla p$ are continuous on finite closed cylinders, with periodic mean-zero pressure. No Sobolev conditions at infinity are involved in that assertion.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-p2-realization"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-p2-realization</summary>

*Proof.* Substitution proves existence of the prescribed solution. Set $w=v-U$. The difference equation is $$w_t+(v\cdot\nabla)w+(w\cdot\nabla)U
    =\nu\Delta w-\nabla p,\qquad \operatorname{div}w=0.$$ The stated velocity regularity implies $w\in C^1L^2\cap CH^2$, so $\frac d{dt}\|w\|_2^2=2\langle w,w_t\rangle$ at each time. In case (iii), the stronger assumptions imply these inclusions. They also imply bounded velocity and spatial derivatives through order two: for $j\le2$, Cauchy–Schwarz in Fourier space uses the finite integral $\int_{\mathbb R^3}|\xi|^{2j}(1+|\xi|^2)^{-4}\,d\xi$.

For the transport term, insert a cutoff $\chi_R$ that equals one on the ball of radius $R$, is supported in the ball of radius $2R$, and satisfies $|\nabla\chi_R|\le C/R$. Its boundary error is at most $CR^{-1}\|v\|_\infty\|w\|_2^2$, which tends to zero. Diffusion is integrated by parts in $H^2$; alternatively its cutoff error is bounded by $CR^{-1}\|w\|_2\|\nabla w\|_2$. The deformation term is bounded by $\|\nabla U\|_{\infty,\mathrm{op}}\|w\|_2^2$.

We justify the pressure pairing according to the stated alternatives. For (i) and (ii), at each fixed time approximate the chosen $H^1$ pressure by compactly supported smooth functions; distributional incompressibility and $w\in L^2$ give $\langle\nabla p,w\rangle=0$. Thus no continuity of $p$ in time is used in (ii). For (iv), integration by parts on the cutoff gives a pressure boundary term bounded by $CR^{-1}\|p\|_2\|w\|_2$, also tending to zero. For (iii), apply Lemma <a href="#lem:b-gradient" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:b-gradient">1</a> directly. Finally, in (v) the equation itself gives at each time $$\nabla p=f-v_t-(v\cdot\nabla)v+\nu\Delta v\in L^2,
 \qquad \|(v\cdot\nabla)v\|_2\le\|v\|_\infty\|\nabla v\|_2.$$ Here $f\in L^2$ follows from the identical estimate for $U$ and its stated time and space regularity. The smooth pressure gradient is curl free, so Lemma <a href="#lem:b-gradient" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:b-gradient">1</a> again cancels the term. This does not choose an $L^2$ representative of the pressure.

In every case the resulting pointwise energy inequality is $$\frac12\frac d{dt}\|w\|_2^2+\nu\|\nabla w\|_2^2
 \le \|\nabla U\|_{\infty,\mathrm{op}}\|w\|_2^2.$$ The coefficient is bounded on each finite interval and $w(0)=0$, so an integrating factor gives $w=0$. The pressure has already disappeared before time integration; the proof imposes no unlisted temporal pressure norm. Substitution then gives $\nabla p=0$. A spatially constant $L^2$ function on $\mathbb R^3$ is zero, which proves the normalization assertion.

On the torus every integration by parts is periodic and has no boundary term. The specified classical regularity justifies norm differentiation, diffusion, and pressure cancellation. The same energy inequality gives uniqueness and the mean-zero normalization fixes pressure. In either domain, the smooth reference velocity and its bounded spatial derivative give unique material trajectories; bounded speed prevents finite-time escape. Solving backwards on a finite interval gives the inverse flow, and smooth ODE dependence gives its smoothness. ◻

</details>

Cases (i)–(v) identify the precise hypotheses used by the applications; their presence in a common lemma does not replace a theorem’s declared comparison class by a different one. In particular, a pressure norm is not silently imposed in a gradient-only application. The proof is the classical energy comparison for prescribed smooth solutions (Leray 1934, sec. 18) <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[Leray1934](#ref-Leray1934)], with its noncompact pressure pairings made explicit.

For the flow $\Phi_t$, differentiation of its trajectory equation gives $$U(t)=(\partial_t\Phi_t)\circ\Phi_t^{-1},\qquad
 \partial_{tt}\Phi_t(a)=
 (f-\nabla p+\nu\Delta U)(t,\Phi_t(a)).$$ These identities describe the material form of the same constructed solution; they do not add an independent existence claim.

<a id="stmt-lem-p2-chart"></a>
<div id="lem:p2-chart" class="lemma">

**Lemma 3** (Physical scaling). *For $a,b>0$, $x=x_0+ay$, and $s=bt$, let $U(t,x)=abV(bt,(x-x_0)/a)$. Then trajectories are transformed by this change of variables, incompressibility is preserved, and at the prescribed physical viscosity $\nu$, $$\mathcal F_\nu[U](t,x)=ab^2\left[
 V_s+(V\cdot\nabla_y)V-\frac{\nu}{a^2b}\Delta_yV
 \right](bt,(x-x_0)/a).$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-p2-chart"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-p2-chart</summary>

*Proof.* The trajectory equation gives $\dot x=abV$; differentiating in space gives $\operatorname{div}_xU=b\operatorname{div}_yV$. The three residual terms scale by $ab^2,ab^2$, and $b/a$, respectively, proving the formula. ◻

</details>

Thus a side-$10$ torus is kept as a physical side-$10$ torus. If a chart is rescaled, the residual is recomputed with the fixed $\nu$; the equation is not identified with a differently normalized viscosity.

<a id="stmt-prop-projection-l2"></a>
<div id="prop:projection-l2" class="proposition">

**Proposition 4** (Projection on a flat torus). *Let $g$ be a smooth force on $[0,\infty)\times\mathbb T_L^3$ with zero spatial mean at each time. There is a unique smooth mean-zero potential $\phi$ satisfying $\Delta\phi=\operatorname{div}g$. Put $Qg=\nabla\phi$ and $Pg=g-Qg$. Then $Pg$ is solenoidal and mean zero, and replacing $(g,p)$ by $(Pg,p-\phi)$ preserves the velocity and the pressure normalization.*

<a id="eq:projection-estimate"></a>
*Use normalized spatial measure, frequencies $\xi_k=2\pi k/L$, and norms $$\|G\|_{H^s}^2=\sum_{k\in\mathbb Z^3}(1+|\xi_k|^2)^s|\widehat G(k)|^2,
 \qquad
 \|G\|_{C^r}=\max_{|\beta|\le r}\|\partial_x^\beta G\|_\infty.$$ For all integers $h,r\ge0$, at each time, $$\label{eq:projection-estimate}
 \begin{aligned}
 \max\{\|\partial_t^h Pg\|_{C^r},\|\partial_t^h Qg\|_{C^r}\}
     &\le S_L\|\partial_t^h g\|_{H^{r+2}},\\
 \|\partial_t^h\phi\|_{C^{r+1}}
     &\le S_Ld_L\|\partial_t^h g\|_{H^{r+2}},
 \end{aligned}$$ where $S_L=(1+52(L/(2\pi))^4)^{1/2}$ and $d_L=(1+(L/(2\pi))^2)^{1/2}$.*

*Uniform bounds, temporal $L^2$ bounds on spatial suprema, and qualitative uniform decay of all mixed derivatives pass from $g$ to $Pg,Qg,\phi$. A quantitative envelope for time order $h$ and output spatial order $r$ passes to $Pg,Qg$ through order $r$ and to $\phi$ through order $r+1$ when the input derivatives through spatial order $r+2$ satisfy that envelope. The constants may depend on the derivative order. Periodicity and stationarity, including either property on a time tail, are preserved on their respective intervals.*

*For computable $L>0$, effective mixed-derivative evaluation and effective derivative bounds for $g$ on finite time slabs give effective evaluation and such bounds for $Pg,Qg,\phi$. Corresponding supplied global input bounds give effective global bounds in each assertion above.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-prop-projection-l2"><summary>Proof of stmt-prop-projection-l2</summary>

<a id="eq:projection-tail"></a>
*Proof.* For $k\ne0$ define $$\widehat\phi(t,k)=-\frac{i\xi_k\cdot\widehat g(t,k)}{|\xi_k|^2},
 \qquad \widehat\phi(t,0)=0.$$ The nonzero-frequency symbols of $Q$ and $P$ are complementary orthogonal projections. Their $H^s$ operator norms are at most one, while the multiplier for $\phi$ has norm at most $d_L$ from $H^s$ to $H^{s+1}$. For $|\beta|\le r$, Cauchy–Schwarz bounds the absolute sum of the Fourier series for $\partial_x^\beta G$ by $$\left(\sum_{k\in\mathbb Z^3}(1+|\xi_k|^2)^{-2}\right)^{1/2}
 \|G\|_{H^{r+2}}.$$ The shell $|k|_\infty=n\ge1$ contains $24n^2+2\le26n^2$ points. Since $\sum_{n\ge1}n^{-2}\le2$, the factor is at most $S_L$. For the tail $|k|_\infty>N\ge1$, it is at most $$\label{eq:projection-tail}
 \left(26(L/(2\pi))^4/N\right)^{1/2}.$$ Applying these estimates to the multipliers proves <a href="#eq:projection-estimate" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:projection-estimate">[eq:projection-estimate]</a>, including the estimate for $\phi$ with one additional spatial derivative. On compact time slabs these tails converge uniformly for every mixed derivative. Thus the series define smooth fields and may be differentiated term by term. The Fourier identities give the Poisson equation, uniqueness of its mean-zero solution, and $\operatorname{div}Pg=0$. Also $-\nabla(p-\phi)+Pg=-\nabla p+g$.

<a id="eq:projection-derivative-bounds"></a>
For every integer $n\ge0$, the multinomial expansion and Parseval give $$\label{eq:projection-derivative-bounds}
 \|G\|_{H^n}^2
 \le\sum_{|\beta|\le n}
       \frac{n!}{(n-|\beta|)!\,\beta!}
       \|\partial_x^\beta G\|_\infty^2.$$ Use $n=r+2$ and $G=\partial_t^h g$. This finite sum proves the uniform bounds and qualitative decay assertions. If its input suprema are bounded by $C_{h,\beta}a_h(t)$, it gives the same envelope $a_h$ for the output, with the constants specified by <a href="#eq:projection-estimate" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:projection-estimate">[eq:projection-estimate]</a>–<a href="#eq:projection-derivative-bounds" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:projection-derivative-bounds">[eq:projection-derivative-bounds]</a>. Integrating the squared estimates proves the temporal $L^2$ assertion from the corresponding input norms. The operators act at each time, so they preserve the stated temporal symmetries.

For effective evaluation, the supplied derivative bounds and <a href="#eq:projection-tail" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:projection-tail">[eq:projection-tail]</a> give a computable Fourier truncation on each finite time slab. The finitely many coefficient integrals are computed by Riemann sums. After scaling the torus to $[0,1]^3$, the integrand for $\widehat G(k)$ has Lipschitz bound $$L\sqrt3\max_j\|\partial_{x_j}G\|_\infty
       +2\pi|k|\|G\|_\infty.$$ This gives an effective quadrature error and hence evaluation of every mixed derivative of all three fields. The displayed finite sums also compute output bounds from the supplied input bounds. ◻

</details>

The estimates apply to the pressure shift $\phi$; in the constructions with original pressure zero, the projected pressure is $-\phi$. Projection generally changes pressure. It supplies no compact-support conclusion on Euclidean space and is distinct from the direct zero-pressure solenoidal shear construction.

<a id="pm-h-6"></a>
###### Effective flat profiles.

<a id="eq:p2-step"></a>
For later cutoff formulas put $$\label{eq:p2-step}
 \rho(s)=\begin{cases}e^{-1/s},&s>0,\\0,&s\le0,\end{cases}
 \qquad \sigma(s)=\frac{\rho(s)}{\rho(s)+\rho(1-s)}.$$ Positive-side derivatives of $\rho$ are polynomials in $1/s$ times $e^{-1/s}$ and extend flatly at zero. The estimate $v^me^{-v}\le(m+k)!v^{-k}$ gives effective error bounds near the seam, and $\rho(s)+\rho(1-s)\ge e^{-2}$. Products, translations, rescalings, and derivatives therefore yield effective cutoffs with all requested derivative bounds. Periodization uses zero collars; at approximate real arguments a finite superset of possible translates is evaluated. No real equality test at a seam or execution of the encoded computation is used.

<a id="sec:bb-boxes"></a>
### Transporting complete boxes

We allow an instruction to change all three side lengths while preserving their product. The geometric question is concrete: given finitely many disjoint closed boxes and prescribed determinant-one affine maps onto another disjoint family, can one carry out every map by a smooth incompressible motion? The two families may overlap each other. Consequently a destination cannot be occupied until every source that obstructs it has been removed.

We first extend an explicitly separated motion to a velocity field. We then give two ways to produce that motion. Separate horizontal projections permit a simple lift and transfer. For general solid boxes we use parking and detours around the other occupied boxes. Figure <a href="#fig:bb-routing" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="fig:bb-routing">1</a> contrasts the height separation with the evacuation order that makes overlapping source and target families harmless.

<a id="pm-h-8"></a>
#### An affine motion and its curl extension

Throughout, a smooth switch is obtained by translating and rescaling $\sigma$ in <a href="#eq:p2-step" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:p2-step">[eq:p2-step]</a>, with its transition strictly inside its allotted interval. Thus successive motions are stationary on neighborhoods of their joining times.

<a id="stmt-lem-bb-curl"></a>
<div id="lem:bb-curl" class="lemma">

**Lemma 5** (A velocity on a neighborhood of each moving box). *Let $K_i(t)=c_i(t)+D_i(t)K_i^0$, $1\le i\le N$, where $K_i^0$ is an axis-parallel box, possibly with zero side lengths, and $D_i(t)$ is positive diagonal with determinant one. Suppose the centers and scales are smooth and constant near the ends of a compact time interval. For the effective conclusion, assume computable endpoints for the initial boxes $K_i^0$, effective evaluation of the centers’ and scales’ derivatives, bounds for those derivatives, and positive lower bounds for every scale on that interval. If the coordinatewise $2\eta$ enlargements of the moving boxes are pairwise disjoint and bounded, for a known $\eta>0$, a compactly supported smooth divergence-free field realizes these motions on open neighborhoods of the initial boxes. It vanishes near the time endpoints. When all enlarged boxes lie in an interior torus chart, the field extends to a mean-zero field on that torus. The field is effective under the effective-data assumptions above; smooth existence itself does not require computable data.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-bb-curl"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-bb-curl</summary>

<a id="eq:bb-potential"></a>
*Proof.* Choose a smooth cutoff $\chi_i$ which is one on the $\eta$ enlargement of $K_i(t)$ and supported in its $2\eta$ enlargement, using products of the effective switches. Put $y=x-c_i$ and $L_i=\dot D_iD_i^{-1}$; then $\operatorname{tr}L_i=0$. Take the curl of $$\label{eq:bb-potential}
 \chi_i\{\tfrac12\dot c_i\times y+\tfrac13(L_i y)\times y\}.$$ The identity $\nabla\times(G\times y)=2G-y\operatorname{div}G+(y\cdot\nabla)G$ shows that this curl equals $\dot c_i+L_i y$ on the plateau. Disjoint supports permit summation over $i$. The path $c_i(t)+D_i(t)D_i(t_0)^{-1}(x_0-c_i(t_0))$ has exactly that derivative and remains in its prescribed box. ODE uniqueness proves the formula. On the compact time interval the normalized matrix norm has a finite bound $M$, computable under the effective-data assumptions; an initial perturbation less than $\eta/(2M)$ remains on the plateau. This proves the neighborhood claim, also for zero-width initial boxes. The potential is zero near time endpoints because the centers and scales are constant there. Its support is bounded. Within a torus chart extend the potentials by zero; periodic curls have zero integral. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-prop-bb-realization"></a>
<div id="prop:bb-realization" class="proposition">

<a id="eq:bb-residual"></a>
**Proposition 6** (Fluid realization and a pressure refinement). *Fix $\nu>0$. A finite collection of the preceding motions, repeated with period one, or preceded by one finite loading interval, yields a smooth prescribed velocity $U$ with $U(0)=0$. Suppose its spatial support is contained in one compact set in $\mathbb R^3$, or its potentials are contained in interior torus charts. Then $$\label{eq:bb-residual}
 f=U_t+(U\cdot\nabla)U-\nu\Delta U$$ has the same support and time periodicity and bounded mixed derivatives of every order. The solution is $(U,0)$, with the uniqueness and material-flow conclusions of Lemma <a href="#lem:p2-realization" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:p2-realization">2</a>. In $\mathbb R^3$ its uniqueness statement also holds when the competing pressure has an $H^1$ representative at each time, without requiring continuity into $H^1$, while the other velocity hypotheses of Section <a href="#sec:model" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:model">2</a> are retained. On the torus $U$ and $f$ have zero mean. Under the effective-data assumptions of Lemma <a href="#lem:bb-curl" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:bb-curl">5</a>, the formula is $f_0+\nu f_1$ with effective coefficients; evaluation is effective for computable $\nu$, and relative to $\nu$ otherwise.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-prop-bb-realization"><summary>Proof of stmt-prop-bb-realization</summary>

*Proof.* The residual identity and Lemma <a href="#lem:b-comparison" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:b-comparison">[lem:b-comparison]</a> give existence, uniqueness and the material flow. In the whole-space pressure refinement use case (ii): the reference field has compact support and smooth bounded mixed derivatives, hence belongs to $CH^2\cap C^1L^2$ with bounded velocity and gradient; the competing velocity and pointwise $H^1$ pressure are exactly that case’s hypotheses. No pressure continuity in time is added. Integrating the residual on a torus uses $\int U=0$, $\int\Delta U=0$ and $\int(U\cdot\nabla)U=\int\operatorname{div}(U\otimes U)=0$.

Each derivative of $e^{-1/s}$ for $s>0$ is a polynomial in $1/s$ times $e^{-1/s}$. The estimate $v^m e^{-v}\le(m+k)!v^{-k}$ supplies effective endpoint error bounds, and the denominator defining $\sigma$ is at least $e^{-2}$. Under those effective-data assumptions all positive scales have effective positive lower bounds. Finite compositions, differentiation, and the logarithms of positive computable scales are therefore effective. At an approximate time, a coarse bound gives a finite superset of possible translates of the zero-extended unit template. This evaluates the periodic field without deciding whether time is an integer. None of these operations iterates the machine execution: the program contains the entire finite instruction list. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-9"></a>
#### Three geometric schedules

For later use we describe the exact hypotheses of the schedules. First suppose $P_i,Q_i\subset\mathbb R^2$ are finite families of closed rectangles, possibly with zero side lengths, separately pairwise disjoint, with affine maps $$F_i(x)=q_i+\operatorname{diag}(\lambda_i,\lambda_i^{-1})(x-p_i),
 \qquad \lambda_i>0,$$ where $p_i,q_i$ are their centers, and require $F_i(P_i)=Q_i$. Their side lengths have a common finite bound. Source and target rectangles belonging to different families need not be disjoint. The smooth existence statements allow arbitrary real rectangle coordinates and scales. For the effective conclusions, assume rational rectangle endpoints and rational scales, together with computable chosen heights and vertical half-widths; then a rational side-length bound and positive gap margins can be computed.

<a id="stmt-lem-bb-lift-parking"></a>
<div id="lem:bb-lift-parking" class="lemma">

**Lemma 7** (Separated projections and parking). *These reciprocal maps have the following smooth compactly supported unit-time realizations, stationary on time collars. They are effective under the rational-data assumptions just stated.*

<a id="eq:bb-convex-coordinate"></a>
1.  *For the products $P_i\times[z_0-h,z_0+h]$, with common $h\ge0$ and $z_0\in\mathbb R$, lift all boxes with their source projections fixed, transfer them at sufficiently separated private heights, and lower them with their target projections fixed. The middle motion may use $g_i(s)=\lambda_i^s$ or $g_i(s)=1+s(\lambda_i-1)$ and matrix $\operatorname{diag}(g_i,g_i^{-1},1)$, with the center interpolated linearly in $s$. Each horizontal width stays between its endpoint widths. In the linear case every point satisfies $$\label{eq:bb-convex-coordinate}
     X_1(s)=(1-s)X_1(0)+sX_1(1).$$*

2.  *Alternatively, choose disjoint parking rectangles away from both families. Move every source to its parking site before delivering any target, using an upward, horizontal, and downward translation. Reshape at the parking sites, then deliver by the same three translations. This uses $7N$ motions. The same schedule applies to assigned onto positive diagonal determinant-one maps between rational solid boxes whose centers lie in a common horizontal plane and whose source and target horizontal projections are separately disjoint. Require both horizontal widths during reshaping to stay inside their reserved parking rectangles and the travel height above the common plane to exceed twice the largest vertical half-width.*

*Both constructions act on neighborhoods of the whole source boxes.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-bb-lift-parking"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-bb-lift-parking</summary>

*Proof.* In the first construction choose padding smaller than a quarter of the within-family horizontal gaps and choose height differences larger than twice the padded vertical half-width. During lift and descent, horizontal separation suffices. During transfer, vertical separation suffices. The two choices of $g_i$ are positive and monotone between $1$ and $\lambda_i$; their reciprocals are monotone as well. Thus the width claim holds. The affine path is $c_i(s)+\operatorname{diag}(g_i,g_i^{-1},1)(X_0-c_i(0))$. Its first coordinate gives <a href="#eq:bb-convex-coordinate" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:bb-convex-coordinate">[eq:bb-convex-coordinate]</a> exactly for the linear choice. Lemma <a href="#lem:bb-curl" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:bb-curl">5</a> now applies.

For the second construction choose padding smaller than the gaps in each union of source projections with reserved parking rectangles, and of target projections with those rectangles. In the evacuation pass, vertical motion is separated from uncollected sources and parked boxes. During delivery it is separated from already filled targets and boxes still parked. At travel height, the moving box is above every resting box. A shape change stays within its own reserved projection. These are the occupied sets at each actual time; source–target intersection causes no interference because all sources have left before delivery starts. Lemma <a href="#lem:bb-curl" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:bb-curl">5</a> applies to each motion with every other box fixed. Finite concatenation gives the required neighborhood map. ◻

</details>

<figure id="fig:bb-routing">

<figcaption>The two separation mechanisms in Lemma <a href="#lem:bb-lift-parking" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:bb-lift-parking">7</a>. Left: horizontal projections separate the lift and descent, and distinct heights separate the transfer; the second horizontal coordinate is suppressed. Right: a temporal schedule, not a spatial drawing. Every source is removed before any target is occupied, so overlap between the two families is harmless.</figcaption>
</figure>

<a id="pm-figsrc-fig-bb-routing"></a>
**Figure source and visual witness** (`fig:bb-routing`)

 \draw[thick,blue!65!black,->] (.5,0)--(.5,2.4)--(4.2,2.4)--(4.2,.1);
 \draw[thick,orange!80!black,->] (1.6,0)--(1.6,1.3)--(3.1,1.3)--(3.1,.1);
 \node[below] at (1,0) {sources};
 \node[below] at (3.7,0) {targets};
 \node[above] at (2.5,2.6) {Separate height layers};
 \begin{scope}[xshift=5.1cm]
  \node[draw,rounded corners,minimum width=2.6cm] (s) at (1.8,2.8) {all source boxes};
  \node[draw,rounded corners,minimum width=2.6cm] (p) at (1.8,1.1) {all boxes parked};
  \node[draw,rounded corners,minimum width=2.6cm] (t) at (1.8,-.6) {all target boxes};
  \draw[->,thick] (s)--(p) node[midway,right] {evacuate};

<a id="eq:bb-mid-stream-path"></a>
<a id="eq:bb-mid-stream-safe"></a>
There is also a useful stream-function implementation of the first schedule. Suppose $a_i,a_i'$ are the lower left corners of $P_i,Q_i$. At height $z_i$, put $d_i=a_i'-a_i$, $b_i=\log\lambda_i$ and $g_i(s)=a_i+sd_i$. On a neighborhood of the swept rectangle use $$\label{eq:bb-mid-stream-path}
 \xi_i(s)=g_i(s)+\operatorname{diag}(\lambda_i^s,\lambda_i^{-s})(\xi-a_i).$$ The Hamiltonian $$H_i=d_{i1}y-d_{i2}x+b_i(x-g_{i1})(y-g_{i2})$$ generates this motion. Put $\widehat H_i=\chi_iH_i$, where $\chi_i$ is one near the whole sweep and supported in its reserved layer. Use $$\dot s(\partial_y\widehat H_i,-\partial_x\widehat H_i,0).$$ Disjoint height layers separate these cutoffs. Vertical phases use $\nabla\times(0,x\chi_i,0)$, equal to $(0,0,1)$ on their column plateaus. If $M=\max_i(1,\lambda_i,\lambda_i^{-1})$ and the column plateau margins are $\delta,\delta'$, an initial thickening smaller than $\min(\delta/(4M),\delta'/(4M),1/(8M))$ stays on every plateau. Thus the affine formula also holds on open neighborhoods. For points of the original rectangle it has the additional bound $$\label{eq:bb-mid-stream-safe}
 X_1(s)\ge\min(a_{i1},a'_{i1}).$$ The bound follows because the first relative coordinate in <a href="#eq:bb-mid-stream-path" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:bb-mid-stream-path">[eq:bb-mid-stream-path]</a> is nonnegative.

<a id="pm-h-10"></a>
#### Solid boxes with overlapping projections

The preceding lift requires separate horizontal projections. We now remove that restriction; this is needed when all three coordinates encode independent stacks.

<a id="stmt-lem-bb-balanced-box-routing"></a>
<div id="lem:bb-balanced-box-routing" class="theorem">

**Theorem 8** (Expansion, obstacle detours, and delivery). *Let $Q_i=a_i+\prod_{j=1}^3[-h_{ij},h_{ij}]$ and $Q_i'=b_i+\prod_{j=1}^3[-k_{ij},k_{ij}]$ be rational solid boxes, with positive half-widths, each family pairwise disjoint. Suppose $s_{ij}=k_{ij}/h_{ij}$ and $\prod_js_{ij}=1$. An effective compactly supported smooth solenoidal field, zero outside the time interval $[1/4,3/4]$, has time-one map $$a_i+y\longmapsto b_i+\operatorname{diag}(s_{i1},s_{i2},s_{i3})y$$ on a neighborhood of each $Q_i$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-bb-balanced-box-routing"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-bb-balanced-box-routing</summary>

*Proof.* <span id="proof:bb-balanced-box-routing" label="proof:bb-balanced-box-routing"></span> For an empty family take the zero field. Otherwise let $R=\max(1,h_{ij},k_{ij})$. Choose a rational $\Lambda>10$ so that centers in each of the lists $(\Lambda a_i)$ and $(\Lambda b_i)$ are separated by more than $20R$ in maximum norm. This is possible because distinct disjoint boxes have distinct centers. Let $$K=\max(4\Lambda,|\Lambda a_{i1}|,|\Lambda b_{i1}|),
